Absence of movements of the foetus and also of the labour pains, blue on yellowish-white discolouration of the body, bad smell in her breath and abnormally big abdomen are the symptoms of the woman whose foetus is dead inside. ( 8 )

# मानसागन्तुभिर्मातुरुपतापैः प्रपीडितः । गर्भो व्यापद्यते कुक्षौ व्याधिभिश्च निपीडितः ।। ९ ।।

Death of the foetus may take place even due to mental disorders, external injury, excessive physical strain for the mother and as an effect of many diseases. (9) योनिसंघरणं सङ्गः कुक्षौ मक्कूल पव च । हृन्युः स्त्रियं मूढगर्मां यथोक्ताश्युपद्रवाः ।। १० ॥ (म. नि. अ. ९)

Yonisanıvarana ( total obstruction of the uterus ), kukşisanga ( dead foetus staying in the uterus for long time ) and makkalla ;( pain after delivery ) developing as complications of mudhagarbha are going to kill the woman. ( 10 )

# ( वायुः प्रकुपितः कुर्यात् संरुध्य रुधिरं स्न्तम् । सूताया हृच्छिरोबस्तिश्लं मक्कलसंब्रकम् ॥ १ ॥ )

ानि श्रीमाधवकरविरचित्रे माधवनिद्यानं मुद्रगर्भनिदानं ममाप्तम् ॥ ६४ ॥

( Makkalla is defined as a disease produced by increased vata causing obstruction to the discharge of uterine fluid after delivery accompanied with pain in the region of the heart, head and urinary bladder. )

Thus ends the chapter on Mūdhagarbha.

# अथ स्त्रतिकारोगनिदानम्

# SŪTIKĀROGA NIDĀNAM ( DISEASE OF PEURPERUM )

# अक्रमदों ज्वरः कम्पः पिपासा गुरुगात्रता । शोधः शूलातिसारौं च सूतिकारोगलक्षणम् ।। १ ॥

General malaise, fever, tremors ( chills ), thirst, fecling of heaviness of the body, swelling, pain in the abdomen and diarrhoca are the symptoms of Sutika roga ( peurperal disorders ). { 1 )

# मिथ्योपवारात् संक्केशाद्विषमाजीर्णमोजनात् । सूतिकायाध्य ये रोगा जायन्ते दारुणास्तु ते ।। २ ॥ ज्वरातीसारशोधाश्च शूलानाहबल्रक्षयाः । तन्द्रारुनिधन्नेकाद्याः कफवातामयोद्भवाः ।। ३ ॥ ज्यसम्बद्धसम्बद्धाः हि ते रोगाः क्षीणमांसबल्धांन्निः । ते सर्वे सूतिकानास्या रोगास्ते चाध्युपद्रवाः ।। ४ ॥

इनि श्रीमाधवत्ररविरचित्ते माधवनिष्ठाः मूनिकारोगनिदानं भमासम् ॥ ६५ ॥ ----------------------

These diseases develop in women who, soon after delivery, indulge in unhea-Ithy activities ( improper management of peurperium ), physical strain, improper foods and drinks and use of uncooked foods.

Fever, diarrhoea, oedema, pain and distention of the abdomen, debility, stupor, loss of appetitie, excessive salivation and other disorders caused by increase of kapha and vata also manifest and are to be considered as difficult to treat in view of poor strength of the boly and of digestive activity. All these diseases derive the name as Sūtika rogas or may even appear as its complications. ( 2-4 )

Thus ends the chapter on Sutika Roga.

# अथ स्तनरोगनिदानम्

# STANAROGA NIDANAM ( DISEASES OF THE BREAST ) सक्षीरौ वाऽप्यदुग्धौ वा प्राप्य दोषः स्तनौ स्त्रियाः । प्रदूध्य मांसरुधिरं स्तनरोगाय कव्यते ॥ १ ॥

Dosas undergoing increase and getting localised in the breasts during the period of lactation or in other periods also, produce many diseases of the breasts. (1)

# पञ्चानामपि तेषां हि रक्तजं विद्र्थि विना । ल्रक्षणानि समानानि बाह्यविद्रश्चिलक्षणैः ॥ २ ॥

इति श्रीमाधवकरविररचिते माधवनिदाने स्तनरोगनिदानं समाप्तम् ॥ ६६ ॥

The five kinds of diseases of the breasts ( produced by the increase of vata, pitta, kapha, all the three together and external agents ) will be having similar symptoms as described for the corresponding types of bahya vidradhi ( abcesses ) excluding of course, the raktaja vidradhi. ( 2 )

Thus ends the chapter on Stana Roga.

# अथ स्तन्यद् विनिद्यनम्

# STANYA DUŞȚI NIDANAM ( DISORDERS OF BREAST MILK ) विशस्तेष्वपि गात्रेषु यथा शुकं न दृश्यते । सर्वदेहाश्रितत्वाच्छ शुक्लक्षणमुच्यते । १ ॥ तदेव चेष्टयुवतेदेशनारस्मरणादपि । शब्दसंश्रवणात्स्पर्शात्संहर्षांच प्रवर्तते ।। २ ॥

Just as sukra ( reproductive element of the male ) though described as present all over the body cannot be seen even if any part of the body is cut open, but comes out of the body of its own accord by the sight, remembrance, hepaing or touch of the woman loved, sheer pleasure of the mind being the cause for its discharge. Similar is the case with stanya ( breast milk ). ( 1-2 )

# सुप्रसन्नं मनस्तत्र हर्षणे हेतुरुञ्यते । आद्वाररसयोनित्वादेवं स्तन्यमपि स्न्रियाः ॥ ३ ॥ तदेवापत्यसंस्पर्शाद्दर्शनात्स्मरणाद् पि ।

# प्रहणाण शरीरस्य शुक्रवरसंप्रवर्तते । स्न्हेद्वो निरन्तरस्तत्र प्रस्नवे देतु रूज्यते ॥ ४ ॥

Even though breast milk is influenced by food, (gets prepared and becomes available in sufficient quantity after the delivery of the child ) it will flow freely by the touch, sight, remembrance and fondling of the baby just as semen, from the body of males. The cause of continuous lactation is the love of the mother towards her child. ( 3-4 )

# गुरुमिर्विविधैरश्रैदुष्टैदौषैः प्रदूबितम् । क्षीरं मातुः कुमारस्य नानारोगाय कल्पते ।। ५ ॥

Indulgence in indigestable and other types of foods and improper activities which bring about increase of the dosas in the body also bring about abnormalities in the breast milk which becomes the cause for many diseases of the child. ( 5 )

# कषायं सलिलप्लप्लाधि स्तन्यं मारुतदूषितम् । कट्वम्लल्रवणं पीतराजीमत् पित्तसंस्रितम् ॥ ६ ॥ कफदुष्टं घनं तोये निमज्जति सपिच्छलम् । द्विलिकूं द्वन्द्रजं विद्यात् सर्वोलिक्षं शिद्दोषजम् ॥ ७ ॥

Milk vitiated by the increase of vata, will be astringent in taste and floats on water; that vitiated by pitta will be pungent, sour or salty in taste and appears as having yellowish threads; vitiated by kapha, it will be thick, sinks in water and sticky; combination of two or three dosas together will produce their corresponding symptoms. ( 6-7 )

# अबुधं चान्दुनिक्षितमेक्ति पाण्डुरम् । मधुरं चाविषणं च प्रसन्नं तत् प्रशस्यते ॥ ८ ॥

इति श्रीमाधवकरविरचिते माधवनिदाने स्तन्यदुष्टिनिदानं समाप्तम् ॥ ६७ ।।

Breast milk is said to be normal when it is found to mix evenly with water, is slightly yellowish white in colour and sweet in taste. ( 8 )

Thus ends the chapter on Stanya Dusti.

# अथ बालरोगनिदानम्

#### BĀLA ROGA NIDĀNAM ( DISEASES OF CHILDREN )

## वातदुष्ठं शिशुः स्तन्यं पित्रन् वातगदातुरः । क्षामस्वरः कुशाक्कः स्याद्भुः स्याद्भूद्वविष्मूत्रमारुतः ।। १ ॥

The child which drinks mich is vitiated by increase of vata in the lactating mother, will become afflicted with diseases of vata origin. It will develop feeble voice and emaciation. ( 1 )

## स्विन्नो भिन्नमलो बालः कामलापित्तरोगवान् । तृष्णालुरुण्णसर्वाङ्गः पित्तदुष्टं पयः पिबन् ॥ २ ॥

Drinking milk which is vitiated by pitta will make the child afflicted with diseases of pitta origin, it will have perspiration, diarrhoea, jaundice, thirst and increased body temperature. ( 2 )

# कफदुष्ठं पिबन् क्षीरं लालालुः श्लेष्मरोगवान् । निद्रान्वितो जडः श्रुनवकाक्षश्रद्धनः शिशुः ॥ ३ ॥

Drinking milk which is vitiated by increased kapha, will make the child afflicted by diseases of kapha origin, it will have excessive salivation and sleep, is inactive, develops swelling of the face and eyes and vomitting. ( 3 )

#### द्धन्द्रजे द्वन्द्रज्ञं रूपं सर्वजे सर्वलक्षणम् ।

· Milk vitiated by the increase of two or all the three dosas will have their respect ve symptoms together.

# शिशोस्तीवामतीवा य रोदनालुक्षयेद्रजम् ॥ ४ ॥ स्त यं स्पृशेद्भूशं देशं यत्र च स्पर्शनाक्षमः । तत्र विद्याद्रुजं, मूधि रुजं चाक्षिनिमीलनात् ॥ ५ ॥ कोष्ठे विबन्धवमधुस्तनदंशान्त्रकूजनेः । आध्मानपृष्टुनमनजठरोन्नमनैर्रवि ॥ ६ ॥ बस्तौ गुह्ये च विष्मूत्रसंगत्रासदिगीक्षणः। स्रोतांस्यद्गानि सन्धीश्च पदयेद्यत्तान्मुहुम् हुः ।। ७ ।।

Pain or discomfort in children should be recognised by its severe or mild crying respectively. The part of the body which the child touches by its hands or the part which the child does not allow touching by others is to be recognised as the painful part or part affected by diseases. Headache or other diseases of the head is to be understood by the closure of the eyes by the child; pain or diseases of the abdomen by the presence of constipation, vomitting, biting of the breast, gurgling noise in the intestines, abnormal distention, bending backward or forward by the abdomen; pain or diseases of the urinary bladder or rectum by suppression of urine

or feces, fright and unsteady vision; the physician should also carefully observe, frequently the orifices, parts, joints and other places of the body of the child ( 4-7)

# कुकुणकः क्षीरदोषाच्छिश्रूनामेव वर्त्मनि । जायते तेन तन्नेत्रं कण्डूरं च स्ववेन्मुद्धः ।। ८ ।। शिशुः कुर्याल्ललाटास्क्रिकूटनासावधर्षणम् । शक्तो नार्कप्रमां द्रष्टुं न वत्मोन्मीलस्थः ।। ९ ॥

Kukūnaka is a disease of the eye-lids of children due to the effect of vitiated breast milk. The eyes will be having itching and exudation; child will be found rubbing its forehead, eyes and round-about areas and nose, it is incapable of seeing sunlight nor opening of the lids. { 8-9 }

# मात्: कुमारो गर्भिण्याः स्तन्यं प्रायः पिवन्नपि । कासान्निसादवमथुतन्द्राकाश्योरुचिभ्रमैः ॥ १० ॥ युज्यते कोष्ठवृद्ध्या च तमाहुः पारिगर्मिकम् । रोगं परिभवाख्यं च युञ्ज्यात्तत्रांग्रदीपनम् ॥ ११ ॥

Children drinking milk of the mother, who has concurrently become pregnant, such children will devclop cough, poor digestive ability, vomitting, stupor, emaciation, loss of appetite, giddiness and enlargement of the abdomen. This disease is known as Parigarbhika or Paribhava and should be treated with drugs which increase the digestion. ( 10-11 )

# तालुमांसे कफः कुद्धः कुरुते तालुकण्टकम् । तेन तालुप्रदेशस्य निम्नता मूर्धि जायते ।। १२ ॥ तालुपातः स्तनव्रेषः कृच्छ्रात् पानं शक्दूद्द्वम् । नृङक्ख्यम् । तृडश्लिकण्डास्यद्धरता वमिः ॥ १३ ॥

Kapha undergoing increase in the muscles of the palate of the child produce Talukantaka. The palate develops a depression in its upper curvature or hangs I with and will have aversion to breasts, drinks with difficulty, has watery stools, thirst, pain in the eyes, neck ( threat ) and mouth, inability to hold the neck ( head ) upright and vomitting. ( 12-13 )

# विसर्वस्तु शिशोः प्राणनाशनो बस्तिशार्षजः । पद्मवर्णो महापद्मनामा दोषभ्रयोद्भवः ॥ १४ ॥ शङ्काम्यां इदयं याति हृदयाद्धा गुदं वजेत् ।

Visarpa ( erysepelas ) which develops in children on the head and the urinary bladder, due to increase of all the three dosas together will be red in colour as that of a lotus petal and so is called as Mahāpadma. It may also appear in the temples spreading to the chest and from the chest to the rectum. This is a fatal disease. ( 14 )

# क्षुद्ररोगे च कथिते त्वजगव्ह्यद्विपूतने ।। १५ ॥

Ajagallika and Ahipūtana-the two diseases described under kşudrarogas will also manifest in children commonly. ( 15 )

ज्वराद्या व्याध्यः संवं महतां ये पुरेरिताः । बाल्हदेहेज्ञपि ते तद्वद्भिन्नेयाः कुशालैः सदा ॥ १६ ॥

Iwara ( fever ) .and all other major diseases will also affect children and should be recognised by the learned ( physicians ) by their specific symptoms. ( 16 )

# क्षणाद्रद्विजते बालः क्षणात्रस्यति रोदिति । नखैर्दन्तैर्दारयति धार्श्रीमान्मानमेव वा ॥ १७ ॥ ऊर्वं निरीक्षते दन्तान् खादेत् कूजाति जम्मते । भ्रवौ क्षिपति दन्तौष्ठं फेनं वमति चासकृत् ॥ १८ ॥ क्षामोऽति निशि जागति शूनाक्षो भिन्नविद्यस्वरः । मांसशोणितगन्ध्य न चाश्राति यथा पुरा ।। १९ ॥ सामान्यं ग्रहजुष्टानां लक्षणं समुदाहृतम् ।

Child becoming suddenly emotional { irritable }, fretful ( afflicted by fear ) and begins to cry without any apparent cause, scratches the mother or its own self, has an upward stare, bites it teeth, makes a moan, yawns more, frowns with the brows, licks the gums and lip, emits froth in vomitting, becomes emaciated, does not sleep at nights, with eyes swollen, loose motions, broken voice, body emiting bad smell like that of flesh and blood and not taking food as before these are the general symptoms seen in cases of possession by Grahas ( evil spirits ). ( 17-19 )

## प्रकनैत्रस्य गात्रस्य खावः स्पन्दनकम्पनम् ॥ २० ॥ ऊध्वं दृष्ट्या निरीक्षेत वकास्यो रक्तगन्धिकः । दन्तान् खादति वित्रस्तः स्तन्यं नैवाभिनन्दति ॥ २१ ॥ स्कन्द्रग्रहगृहीतानां रोदनं चाल्पमेव च ।

If the child is possessed by Skandagraha, it will be having flow of tears in one eve, heavy perspiration, shivering and convulsions, gaze fixed up, irregularity of the face, smell of blood from the mouth, biting of the teeth, fretfulness, does not relish the breast milk and cries mildly. ( 20-21 )

# नष्ठसंश्रो वमेत् फेनं संक्षावानतिरोदिति । पूर्यशोणितगन्धित्वं स्कन्दापस्मारत्कक्षणम् ॥ २२ ॥

Frothy vomitting and fainting and crying too much on gaining consciousness, body emitting the smell of pus and blood are the features of the child possessed by Skandāpasmāra. ( 22 )

# स्नस्ताक्लो भयचकितो विद्यद्गगन्धिः । सास्त्राववणपरिपीडितः समन्तात् । स्फोटेश्व प्रचिततन्द्रः सदाहुपाकैविज्ञेयो भवति शिश्रुः क्षतः शकुन्या ।। २३ ॥

Drooping of the limbs, fretfulness, body emitting of smell of birds, exudative ulcers on the body, skin studded with papules which form pus and having burning sensation are the symptoms of the child possessed by Sakunigraha ( 23 )

# वणः स्फोटैश्चितं गात्रं पङ्कुगम्घं स्ववेदस्त्र् । भिन्नवर्चा उवरी दाही रेवतीश्रहरूक्षणम् ॥ २४ ॥

Skin with boils and ulcers exuding blood having the smell of silt, watery motions, fever and burning sensation in the body are the symptoms in a child possessed by Revatigraha. ( 24 )

# अतीसारो ज्वरस्तृष्णा तिर्यक्रेश्नणरोदनम् । नष्टनिद्रस्तथोद्विस्रो प्रस्तः पूतनया शिशुः ॥ २५ ॥

Diarrhoea, fever, thirst, body bent sidewards, crying, loss of sleep and irritability are seen in the child possessed by Putanagraha. ( 25 )

छदिः कासो ज्वरस्तृष्णः वसागन्धोऽतिरोद्दनम् । स्तन्यद्वेषोऽतिसारश्च अन्धपूतनया भवेत् ॥ २६ ॥

Vomitting, cough, fever, thirst, body emitting smell or muscle fat, with too much of crying, aversion to breast milk and diarrhoea are seen in child possessed by Andhapūtana. ( 26 )

# वेपते कासते क्षीणो नैत्ररोगो विगन्धिता । छर्द्यंतीसारयुक्तश्च शीतपूतनया शिशुः ॥ २७ ॥

Abnormal movements of the body parts, cough, emaciation, disorders of the eye, bad smell from the body, vomitting and diarrhoea are seen in children possessed by Śītapūtana. ( 27 )

# प्रसन्नवर्णवद्गः सिराभिरभिसंवृतः । मूत्रगन्धी च बह्राशी मुखमण्डिकया भवेत् ॥ २८ ॥

Brightness of the face, appearance of veins prominently on the body, smell of urine from the body and child eating too much of food are seen as symptoms of Mukhamandikā graha. ( 28 )

# छदिरप (स्य, न्द्रनकण्ठास्यश्यश्यश्यश्रो षान्धिताः । उद्घर्वं पञ्येद्दशेद्दन्तान् नैगमेषग्रह वदेत् ॥ २९ ॥

Vomitting, visible pulsations in the neck, dryness of the mouth, fainting, bad smell from the body, gaze fixed up and bitting of teeth are seen in children possessed by Naigameya graha. ( 29 )

# प्रस्तब्धाक्षः स्तनद्वेषी मुह्यते चानिशं मुहुः । तं बालमचिराद्धन्दि ग्रहः संपूर्णलक्षणः ।। २० ॥

इति श्रीमाधवकरविरचिते माधवनिदाने बालरोगनिदानं समाप्तम् ॥ ६५ ॥

Loss of movement of the eves, aversion to the breast, frequent faintings and appearance of all the symptoms together are fatal signs in children possessed by evil spirits. ( 30 )

Thus ends the chapter on Bala Rogas.

# अथ विषरोगनिद्यनम्

#### VISA ROGA NIDANAM ( DISEASES DUE TO POI5ON )

# स्थावरं जब्गमं चैव द्विविधं विषमुच्यते । मूलाद्यात्मकमाद्यं स्यात् परं सर्वादिसंभवम् ॥ १ ॥

Poisons are of two kinds; Sthavara and Jañgama. Roots and other parts of plants are Sthavara and poison from animals like snakes are Jangama. ( 1 )

# निद्रां तन्द्रां क्लमं दाहमपाकं लोमहर्षणम् । शोधं चैवातिसारं च जक्कमं कुरुते विषम् ॥ २ ॥

Sleep, stupor, exhaustion, burning sensation, indigestion, horripulations, swelling and diarrhoea are the general symptoms caused by Jañgama Visa ( animal poison ). ( 2 )

# स्थावरं च उवरं हिन्दां दन्तहर्षं गलव्रद्वम् । फेनच्छद्यसंचिश्वासं मूच्छां च कुरुते भूजम् ॥ ३ ॥

Sthavara Visa will generally produce fever, hiccough, thingling of the gums, pain or difficulty in the throat, frothy vomitting, loss of appetite, difficulty in breathing and fainting. ( 3 )

# इक्रिता मनुष्याणां वाक्वेष्ठ।मुखवेकृतैः । ज्ञानीयाद्धिदातारमेभिलिङ्केश्य बुद्धिमान् ॥ ४ ॥ न ददात्युत्तरं पृष्ठो विवक्षुमोहमेति च । अपार्थं बहु संकीण मापते चापि मूढवन् ॥ ५ ॥ हस्तयकस्मात् स्फोटयत्यङ्गलोविलिखेन्महीम् । वेपथुश्र्यास्य भवति व्यस्त्रश्न्योन्यमीक्षते ॥ ६ ॥ विवर्णवक्रो ध्यामश्च नखेः किचिचित्वनस्यपि । आलमेतासनं दोनः करेण च शिरोराद्यम् ॥ ७ ॥

## वर्तते विपरीतं च विषदाता विचेतनः । ( -) क. अ. १ )

An intelligent person ( physician ) can inal out the Visadata ( person who is in the habit of administering poisons to others) by his talk, actions and facial expression; such as-does not quickly answer the question, feigns ignorance, gives wrong and irrelevant replies, talks like a fool, laughs without any reason, makes knuckles from his fingers, scratches the ground by the toes, shivers, fears others, looks at others for support, wears dirty cloths, puts on bad expression on the face, tears cloths, etc., by bis nails, accepts a seat with timidity and hesitation, pulls out his hairs by the hands and behaves in a very unusual manner. ( 4-7 )

उद्घेष्टनं मूलविषैः प्रलापो मोह प्रव च ।। ८ ।। ज्ञम्भणं वेपनं ध्वासो मोहः पत्रविण नु । मुप्कशोधः फल्वविपद्दाहोऽन्नद्वेष एव व ।। ९ ।।

# भवत् पूर्वविषैक्ञ्चिदाध्यानं श्वास एव च । त्वकसारनिर्यासविष्ठवयुक्तैर्मवन्ति हि ।। १० ।। आस्यदौर्गन्ध्यपारुण्यशिरोदक्ककसंस्त्रवाः । फेनागमः क्लीरविविद्वेदो ग्रुगान्नता ।। ११ ।। हृत्यीजनं घातविषैर्मच्छा दाहश्य तालुनि । प्रायेण काल्छघातीनि विषाण्येतानि निदिंशेत् ॥ १२॥ ( मु. क. अ. २ )

Poisonous roots consumed will produce pain all over the body as though hit by a stick, delerium and delusion; poisonous leaves will produce excess yawning, chills, difficulty in breathing and delusion, poisonous fruits will cause swelling of the face, burning sensation and aversion to food; poisonous flowers will cause vomitting, distention of the abdomen and difficulty in breathing; poisonous barks, gum. extract, etc., will produce bad smell in the mouth, roughness of the body, headache and exudation of fluid from mouth, nose, etc, poisonous latex ( milky juice of plants ) will cause froth at the mouth, diarrhoea and feeling of heaviness of the body; poisonous minerals, ores, etc., will produce pain in the region of the heart, fainting and burning sensation in the palate.

All the above kinds of poisons are generally Kalaghati-kill the person after long period of time. ( 8-12 )

> सद्यः क्षतं प्रचयते यस्य जन्तोः स्नवेदक्तं पच्यते चाप्यमीक्ष्णम् । कृष्णीभूतं क्लिस्रमत्यर्थपूर्ति क्षतान्मांसं शीर्यते चापि यस्य ॥ १३ ॥ तृष्णा मूच्छा ज्वरदाहौं च यस्य दिग्धाहरतं तं पुरुषं व्यवस्येत् । लिङ्गान्येतान्येव कुर्यादसित्रवणे विषं यस्य दत्तं प्रमादात ॥ १४ ॥ सपीतं गृद्दधूमाभं पूरीषं योऽतिसार्यते । फेनमुद्रमते चापि विषपीतं तमादिशेन् ॥ १५ ॥ ( मु० क० अ० ३ )

The wound undergoing pus formation immediately, discharges lot of blood again and again and under goes decomposition, black coloured with too much of exudate, emitting foul smell and the surrounding muscles falling off by decomposition are the signs of wounds caused by weapons smeared with poison; the person with such wounds will be having thirst, fainting, fever and burning sensation also in addition.

Sometimes enemies will put poisons into ordinary wounds of persons, or make the wounded person drink poison by force; even in these conditions also the above signs and symptoms will be found. A person who eliminates stools which is yellow or black in colour resembling chimney soot and brings out frothy vomitting is to be considered as administered poison. { 13-15 }

<sup>1.</sup> It is more appropriate to read 'Mukha' instead of Muska in this stanza.

# वातपित्तककात्मानो भोगिमण्डलिराजिल्हाः । यथाकमं समाख्याता, दृध्यन्तरा द्वन्द्रर्त्रपिणः ॥ १६ ॥

Snakes with a hood, those with patches and the ones with stripes are . classified as possessing the properties and qualities of vāta, pitta and kapha respectively. The hybrid snakes will be having mixed qualities of two or more dosas. ( 16 )

# दंशो मोगिकृतः कृष्णः सर्ववातविकारकृत् । पीतो मण्डलिजः शोधो मृदुः पित्तविकारवान् ॥ १७ ॥ राजिलांत्यो मवेद्दशः स्थिरशोधश्च पिच्छिलः । पाण्डुः स्निग्धोऽतिसान्द्रास्त्रक् सर्वश्लेष्मविकारकृत् ॥ १८ ॥

The site of bite of a hooded snake will be black and it causes all the symptoms of vātavrddhi ( increase of vāta ) to manifest; that of the maņḍala ( snake with patches ) will be yellow in colour, with soft swelling and produces all the symptoms of pittavrddhi ( increase of pitta ); that of the rajila ( striped snakes ) will be having a hard swelling, sticky and exudes whitish, sticky and thick blood from the wound and causes the appearance of symptoms of increased kapha. ( 17-18 )

# अश्वत्थदेवायतनद्यमशानवल्मीकसन्ध्यासु चतुरपथेषु । याम्ये च दृष्टाः परिवर्जनीया ऋक्षे सिरामर्मसु ये च दृष्टाः ॥ १९ ॥

Biting by snakes happening near an Aswattha tree ( Ficus religiosa ), in a temple, crematorium and ant hill; in the evenings, at cross roads, in the constellation of bharani and if the bite is on siramarmas ( vital spots on the body having prominence of veins ) should be rejected for treatment. ( 19 )

# दर्वीकराणां विषमाश्रुधाति सर्वाणि चोण्णे द्विगुणीभवन्ति ।

The poison of hooded snakes is an instant killer and that of others becomes twice powerful if associated with heat ( touch of fire, hot objects, fomentation, hot season, etc. ). ( 20 )

# यजीर्णपित्तातपर्याडितेषु बालैषु वृद्धेषु वुभुक्षितेषु ॥ २०॥ क्षीणक्षते मेहिनि कुष्ठयुक्तं रक्षेऽबले गर्भवतीषु चापि ।

Likewise the effect of poison will be doubly powerful in persons suffering from indigestion, diseases due to increase of pitta, exposure to sun; in children and the aged, who are hungry, wounded, sulfering from diabetes and leprosy, who are illnourished, and women, specially the pregnant. ( 20 )

> शख्यक्षते यस्य न रक्तमेति राज्यो लताभिश्च न संभवन्ति ॥ २१ ॥ शीताभिरद्विश्य न रोमहर्षों विषाभिभूतं परिवर्जयेन्तम् । जिह्यं मुखं यस्य च केशशातो नासाय पादश्य सकण्डमद्गः ।। २२ ।। क्रुणः सरक्तः श्वयथुश्य दंशे हन्वोः स्थिरत्वं च विवर्जनीयः । वर्तिर्घना यस्य निरेति वक्राद्रक्तं स्ववेद्र्ध्वमध्य यस्य ॥ २३ ॥

# दृष्टानिपाताश्वतरथ्य यस्य तं चापि वैद्यः परिवर्जयेष्य। उम्मस्तमत्यर्थम्पद्रतं वा हीनस्वरं वाऽप्यथया विवर्णम् ॥ २४ ॥ सारिष्टमन्यर्थमवेगिनं च क्वात्वा नरं कर्म न तत्र कुर्यात् ।

Blood not flowing out even if a cut is made, marks of blow not appearing on the skin even if beaten by a stick or rope, sprinkling of cold water ( on the face ) not causing horripulations ( not arousing him to consciousness ), if found in a person bitten by snakes should be refused treatment.

Irregularity of the face, falling of hairs ( or pulling out the hairs by force by the person himself ) deformity in the nosc, inability to hold the neck straight, black blood oozing out and swelling at the site of bite, stiffness of the lower jaw seen in the patient, he should also be rejected.

Similarly, saliva, coming out of the mouth like a thick wick, blood coming out of the body through both upper and lower orifices, lack of expertise of the physician to remove the poison, such patients should also be refused treatment.

He who has lost the balance of mind, who has many complications, who has feeble voice and discolouration, who has developed signs of death and who is not having the urges of the body ( urge or sensation of urination, defecation, etc., in other words fully senseless ) if seen in a patient, he should also be rejected. ( 21-24 )

# जीर्णं विषधौषधिभिर्हतं वा दावास्विवातातातपशोषितं वा ॥ २५ ॥ स्वभावतो वा गुणविप्रहीनं विषं हि दूषीविषनामुपैति । ( मु. क. अ. २ )

Poisonous substances which are very old, which have been mixed with antipoisonous drugs, which have been dried up by forest fire, breeze and sun-light and those which by nature are not having one or more ( powerful ) properties of poisons-all these are given the name Dusi visa. ( 25 )

# वीर्याल्यभावास्त्र निपातयेज्ञत् कफान्वितं वर्षगणानुबन्धि ॥ २६ ॥ तेनादितो भिन्नपुरीषवर्णां वैगन्ध्यवैरस्ययुतः पिपास्ती । मूच्छौं भ्रमं गद्गद्रद्वाग्वमिं च विचेष्टमानोऽरतिमाप्नुयाद्वा ॥ २७ ॥ ( सु. क. अ. २ )

Because of its poor potency it does not kill the person ( either suddenly or even later }, but gets associated with kapha and remains inside the body without undergoing digestion. The person who is afflicted with such poison will be having diarrhoea, discolouration, bad smell from the body, bad taste in the mouth, thirst, faintings, giddiness, stammering, vomitting, unnatural movements and restlessness. ( 26-27 )

# आमाशयस्थे कफवातरोगी, पक्काशयस्थेऽनिल्छपित्तरोगी । भवेत् समुद्धवस्तशिरोरुद्वाद्गो विल्दुनपक्षस्तु यथा विद्वक्तः ॥ २८ ॥ ( सू. क. अ. २ )

It it is lodged in the stomach, the person becomes a patient of diseases of kapha-vata; if it is in the intestines, he becomes a patient of diseases of vata and pitta, loses all the hairs of the head and the body and appears like a bird whose wings have been pulled off. ( 28 )

# स्थितं रसादिष्वथवा यथोकानू करोति धात्रप्रभवान् विकारान् । कोपं च शीतानिलदुर्दिनेषु यात्याश्रु, पूर्वं श्टणु तस्य रूपम् ॥ २९ ॥ ( सू. क. अ. र )

If it is lodged in the rasa and other dhatus ( tissues ) of the body, it produces those diseases which are likely to develop in those tissues; the symptoms become severe or troublesome by exposure to cold wind and on cloudy days First, note its signs. ( 29 )

# निद्रागुरुत्यं च विजम्भणं च विश्लेषहर्षांवधवाऽक्रमर्दम् । ततः करोत्यस्त्रमदाविषाकावरोचकं मण्डल्ककोठजन्म ॥ ३० ॥ मांसक्षयं पादकरप्रश्रशोधं मूच्छौं तथा छदिंमधातिसारम् । दूधीविषं भ्वास्तर्याउमरांश्य कुर्यात् प्रमृद्धि जठरस्य चापि ।। ३१ ।। ( सु. क. थ. २ )

Excessive sleep, feeling of heaviness of the body, yawning, looseness of joints, horripulations, mild pains in the body, toxicity after meals, indigestion, loss of appetite, appearance of rashes and papules on the skin, emaciation, profound swelling of the feet, fainting, vomitting, diarrhoea, difficulty in breathing, thirst, fever and enlargement of the abdomen-are the symptoms caused by Dusi visa. ( 30-31 )

# उन्माद्मन्य ज्ञनये त्तर्थाऽन्यदानाह्यन्यत्क्षपयेष्ठ शुक्रम् । गाद्गद्रद्यमन्यज्जनयेश्य कुष्ठं तांस्तान्विकारांश्च बहुप्रकारान् ॥ ३२ ॥ ( मु. क. अ. २ )

Some kinds of dusi viṣa will produce insanity, some others enlargement of the abdomen, some other cause loss of semen, yet others cause stammering and some others produce leprosy and many other diseases. ( 32 )

# द्रबितं देशकालान्नदिचास्वप्नेरभीक्णशः । यस्मात् संदूषयेद्धातून तस्माद्रद्र्षोविषं स्मृतम् ॥ ३३ ॥ साध्यमात्मवनः सद्यो याप्यं संवत्सरोतिथतम् । दूषीविषमसाध्यं स्यात् श्लीणस्याद्दितसेविनः ।। ३४ ।। ( सु. क. अ. २ )

Dust visa is so called because it vitiates the dhatus ( tissues ) often and on, influenced and supported by desa ( habitat ), kala ( season ), anna ( food ) and divāsvapana ( day sleep ). It is curable, if it is of recent origin and in persons of strong will; it becomes chronic after one year and it is incurable in persons who are emaciated, weak and who indulge in unhealthy foods and activities. ( 33-34 )

> सौभाग्यार्थ स्त्रियः स्वेदं रजो नानाक्कजान् मल्ठान् । शत्रुप्रयुक्तांश्च गरान् प्रयच्छन्तयन्नमिश्रितान् ॥ ३५ ॥ तैः स्यात् पाण्डुः क्वेशोऽल्पान्निर्गरश्चास्योपजायते । मर्मध्यमनाध्मानं इस्तयोः शोधलक्षणम् ॥ ३६ ॥ जठरं ग्रहणीदोषो यक्ष्मा गुल्मः क्षयो ज्वरः । पवंविधस्य चान्यस्य व्याधेलिक्कानि दर्शयेल् ॥ ३७ ॥

Women expecting wealth ( or other benefits ) or induced by foes, administer artificial poison to men consisting of sweat, powder of the body, menstrual fluid and excretions ( of poisonous or non-poisonous animals ) mixed with food. These poisons are known as Gara visa (homicidal poison). The person becomes pale, emaciated, of poor digestion, pain or other difficulties in vital organs, distention of the abdomen, swelling of the hands, or becomes affected with udara roga ( enlargement of abdomen ), grahani ( deodenal disease ), yaksma ( tuberculosis ), gulma ( abdominal tumor ), kṣaya ( emaciation, consumption ), jwara ( fever ) and other diseases with allied symptoms. ( 35-37 )

# यस्माल्लूनं तृणं प्राप्ता मुनैः प्रस्वेदबिन्दवः । तस्माल्लुतास्तु भाष्यन्ते संख्यया ताश्च षोडदा ॥ २८ ॥ ( मु. क. अ. २ )

The droplets of sweat of the sage ( Vasistha who had become angry with Viswamitra at the abduction of Kamadhenu, the cow ) which fell on the grass become converted into Lutas ( spiders ) which are of sixteen kinds. ( 38 )

> ताभिद्देष्टे दंशकोथः प्रवृत्तिः क्षतजस्य च । ज्वरो दाहोऽतिसारश्च गद्यः म्युश्च विद्योषज्ञाः ॥ ३९ ॥ पिडका विविधाकारा मण्डलानि महान्ति च । शोधा महान्तो मृदवो रक्ताः इयावाश्चलास्तथा ।। ४० ।। सामान्यं सर्वत्दूतानामेतद्दंशस्य लक्षणम् । ( मु. क. अ. २ )

If bitten by these spiders, there will be putrefaction at the site of the bite, flow of blood, fever, burning sensation, diarrhoea and other symptoms caused by the increase of all the three dosas together. Papules of different kinds, big patches, profound swelling which is soft, red or black in colour and movable are generally seen in spider bites. ( 39-40 )

# दंशमध्ये तु यत् कृष्णं द्यावं वा जालकाचितम् ॥ ४१ ॥ ऊर्ध्वाकृति भूशं पाकं क्रोदशोथज्वरान्वितम् । दुषीविषाभिल्रं ताभिस्तदृष्टमिति निर्दिशेत् ॥ ४२ ॥ ( च. चि. अ. २५ )

Black or blue colour at the centre of the bite, appearance of net work, elevation of the skin, severe pus formation, exudation, swelling, fever are the features if bitten by spiders whose poison is said to be slow acting. ( 41-42 )

# शोधः श्वेताः सिता रक्ताः पीता वा पिडका ज्वरः। प्राणान्तिकाश्च जायन्ते श्वासहिकाशिरोग्रहाः ॥ ४३ ॥ ( च. चि. अ. २५ )

Swelling, appearance of papules of white, red or yellow in colour, fever, difficulty in breathing, hiccough, headache are produced quickly if bitten by spiders whose poison is said to be quick acting and fatal. ( 43 )

# अादंशाच्छोणितं पाण्डमण्डलानि ज्वरोऽर्हचिः । लोमहर्षक्ष दाहश्याप्याखुद्रषीविषादिते ॥ ४४ ॥ ( च. चि. अ. २५ )

Bleeding from the site of bite, appearance of white patches on the skin, fever, loss of appelite, horripulation and burning sensation are the symptoms of poisoning caused by Rat bite. ( 44 )

# मूच्छाङ्गशोधवैद्य गर्य क्रेन्द्राब्दा श्रुतिज्वराः । शिरोगुरुत्वं लालास्क्र्छविंश्वासाध्यमूषिकैः ॥ ४५ ॥ ( च. चि. अ. २४ )

Fainting, swelling, discolouration, perspiration, deafness, fever, feeling of heaviness of the head, excess salivation, vomitting of blood, are the incurable symptoms if bitten by a poisonous rat. { 45 }

# कारण्य द्यावत्वमथवा नानावर्णत्वमेष वा । मोहोऽथ वर्चस्तो मेदो दृष्टे स्यात् क्रकलासकैः ॥ ४६ ॥ ( च. चि. अ. १३ )

Black or bluish discolouration or appearance of different colours on the skin, delusion, and diarrhoea are the symptoms caused by the bite of Krkaläsa { chamelion ). { 46 )

# दृहत्यन्निरिवादौ च भिनसीवोध्वमाशु च । वृश्चिकस्य विषं याति दंशे पश्चात्तु तिष्ठति ॥ ४७ ॥ दश्चोऽसाध्यश्च हृद्याणरसनोपहृतो नरः। मांसैः पतद्भिरत्यर्थं वेदनातों जहात्यसून् ।। ४८ ॥ ( च. चि. अ. २५ )

Severe burning sensation as though touched by fire, severe pain as though the part is coming off constant pain at the site are the symptoms of Scorpion sting. If the scorpion is very poisonous, the patient will develop distress in the heart, loss of function of the nose and tongue, falling off of the muscles ( after putrefaction ) and even death by severe pain. ( 47-48 )

# विसर्पः श्वयथुः शूल्रं ज्वरच्छदिरथापि च । लक्षणं कणभैर्दे दंशश्चैवावसीदति ॥ ४९ ॥

Ulcer spreading from place to place, swelling pain, fever, vomitting and rashes at the site of bite are the symptoms if stung by a Kanabha ( wasp). ( 49 \

# दृष्टल्लोमोश्विटिङ्गेन स्तब्धलिङ्गो भृशार्तिमान् । दृष्टः शीतोदकेनैव सिक्तान्यङ्गानि मन्यते ॥ ५० ॥

Horripulations, loss of consciousness and severe pain are the symptoms if bitten by a Ucchitinga ( poisonous crab ). ( 50 )

# पकदंध्रादितः शूनः सरुजः पीतकः सतृद् । छदिंनिद्रः च सविषैमण्डूकैदृश्चलक्षणम् ॥ ५१ ॥ मत्स्यास्तु सविषाः कुर्युर्दाहं शोथं रुजं तथा । कण्डूं शोथं उवरं मूच्छौं सविषास्तु जलौकसः ॥ ५२ ॥

Swelling, pain and yellow discolouration at the site of bite, thirst, vomitting and excessive sleep are the symptoms produced by bite of poisonous frogs,

Bite by poisonous fish will cause burning sensation, swelling and pain; by poisonous leeches will cause itching, swelling, fever and fainting, ( 1-52 )

# विदाहं श्वयथुं तोदं स्वेदं च गृहगोधिका । दंशे स्वेदं रजं दाहरं कुर्याच्छतपदीविषम् ॥ ५३ ॥ कण्डूमान् मशकेरीषच्छोथः स्मान्मन्दवेद्नः । असाध्यकीटसदृशमसाध्यं मशकक्षतम् ॥ ५४ ॥ सद्यःप्रस्नाविणी श्यावा दाहमूच्छांज्वरान्विता । पिडका मक्षिकादंशे तासां तु स्थगिकाऽसुहृत् ॥ ५५ ॥

Bite by house lizard will cause severe burning sensation, swelling, pricking pain and perspiration; bite by the centipede will cause perspiration, pain and burning sensation.

Bite by ordinary musquitoes will cause irritation, slight swelling and mild pain and bitten by poisonous musquitoes, the symptoms similar to that of bite of poisonous insects will develop.

If a bee stings, it produces bleeding, black colour at the site and burning sensation; fainting, fever and papules develop if the bee is of sthe site and burning
bite will be fatal ( 53, 55 ) bite will be fatal. ( 53-55 )

# चतुरपद्भिद्वैद्भव्देश्व नखदश्तविषं च यत् । शूयते पच्यते वापि स्नवति ज्वरयत्वपि ।। ५६ ॥

Swelling, putrefaction and exudation from the site of bite and fever are the symptoms if assaulted by the claws and teeth of animals of four legs or two legs. ( 56 )

श्वश्रुगालतरर्घवर्क्षंब्याघादीनां यदाऽनिलः । श्लेष्मप्रदुष्टो मुष्णाति संक्षां संश्लावद्याश्रितः ॥ ५७ ॥ तदा प्रस्तलाङ्गूलहनुस्कन्धोऽतिलालवान् । अध्यक्तबधिरान्धश्च सोऽन्योन्यमभिधायति ॥ ५८ ॥ प्रमूढोऽन्यतमस्त्वेषां खादन्विपरिधामदति । तेनोन्मत्तेन दृष्टस्य दृष्ट्रिणां सविषेण त् ॥ ५९ ॥ सुप्तता जायते दंशे कृष्णं चातिस्रवत्यस्यस्य । दिग्धविद्धस्य लिङ्गन प्रायशश्चोपल्प्सितः ॥ ६० ॥ येन चापि भवेद्दश्यस्तस्य चेष्टां रतं नरः । बहुशः प्रतिकुर्वाणः कियाहीनो विपक्यति ॥ ६१ ॥ दृष्ट्रिणा येन दृष्ट्र्य तद्रुपं यस्तु पदयति । अप्सु चादशबिम्बे वा तस्य तद्रिष्टमादिशेत ॥ ६२ ॥ त्रस्यत्यकस्माद्योऽभीक्ष्णं दृष्ट्वाऽपि वा जल्त्रम् । जलत्रासं तु तं विद्याद्रिष्ठं तद्वि कीर्तितम् ॥६३॥

Vata undergoing increase and associating with increased kapha, invade and destroy the nervous system of animals such as dogs, fox, hyena, bear, tiger, etc., and makes them rabid. Such rabid animals will have their tail, lower jaw and shoulders drooping down, eliminate large quantities of saliva, become blind and deaf, run amuck everywhere, fight with other animals and bite all others senselessly. If bitten by such rabid animals, there will be loss of sensation at the site of bite, black blood flows out freely, the ulcer will have the symptoms of a wound caused by poisonous weapon, the patient imitates all the actions of the rabid animal, cries or shouts in the same manner, becomes unconscious and dies. Sight of the animal which bit him in water or in the mirror is a fatal sign; the bitten person becoming afraid of sight or touch of water is also a fatal sign known as Jalatrasa ( hydrophobia ). ( 57-63 )

# भद्यो वा जलवासी न कथंचन सिद्ध्यति । प्रसुप्तो वोर्त्थितो वाऽपि स्वस्थस्त्रस्तो न सिद्ध्यति ॥ ६४ ॥

He, who develops this fear of water even though not bitten by rabid animals or who gets it soon after getting up from sleep or who gets it even if he is a healthy person-are all to be considered as incurable. ( 64 )

# प्रशान्तदोषं प्रकृतिस्थधातुमन्नाभिकामं समम्नविदकम् । प्रसन्नवर्णीन्द्रियचित्तचेष्ठं वैद्योऽवगच्छेदविषं मनुध्यम् ॥ ६५ ॥

इति श्रीमाधवकरविरचिते माधवनिदाने विषनिदानं समाप्तम् ॥ ६९ ॥

All the increased dosas getting back to normal, all the tissues being in normal condition, developing desire for food, elimination of urine, feces, etc., properly, having the normal pleasant colour and complexion, functioning of the senses and mind, with normal activities and behaviour-are to be observed by the physician, as those of the person fully relieved of poison. ( 65 )

Thus ends the chapter on Visa.

242

## अथ विषयानुऋमणिका

# ANUKRAMANIKA ( CONTENTS OF THE TREATISE )

ज्वरोऽतिसारो ग्रहणी चार्शोऽजीणे विस्त्रविका । अलस्थ्य विलम्बी च किमिरुक्षाण्डुकामलाः ।। १ ।। इलीमकं रक्तपित्तं राजयक्मा उरःक्षतम् । कासो हिका सह ध्वासैः स्वरभेदस्त्वरोचक: ।। २ ।। छदिस्तृष्णा च मूच्छाद्या रोगाः पानात्ययादयः । दाहोन्मादावपस्मारः कथितोऽधानिलामयः ।। ३ ।। वातरक्तमूरुस्तम्भ आमवासोऽथ शुल्रुक । शूल्मानाह उदावतोऽथ गुल्मरुक 11 ४ 11 पत्किज हृद्रोगो मूत्रकृद्धू च मूत्राघातस्तथाऽधमरी । मधुमेहश्च प्रमेहो पिडकाश्च प्रमेहजाः ॥ ५ ॥ मेदस्तथोदरं शोधो व्यद्धिश्च गलगण्डकः । गण्डमालाऽपची य्रन्थिरर्वुदः श्लीपदं तथा ॥ ६ ॥ द्वौ वर्णौ भग्ननाडिके । विद्रधिवेणशोधश्च भगन्दरोपदंशौ शूक्रदोषस्त्वगामयः ॥ ७ ॥ च शीतपित्तमुदर्दश्च कोठश्चैवाम्लपित्तकम् । विसर्पश्च सविरूफोटः सरोमान्त्यो मसूरिकाः ॥ ८ ॥ श्चुद्रास्यकर्णना स्नाक्षि शिरःस्त्रीबाल्ककामयाः विषं चेत्ययम् हिण्य रुग्विनिश्चयसंग्रहः ।। ९ ॥

Jwara, Atīsāra, Grahaņī, Arśas, Ajīrņa, Visūcikā, Alasaka, Vilambikā, Krimiroga, Pāņḍu, Kāmalā, Halīmaka, Raktapitta, Rājayaksmā, Uraḥkṣata, Kāsa, Hikkā, Swasa, Swarabheda, Arocaka, Chardi. Trsnā, Mūrchā, etc., Pānātyaya, etc., Dāha, Unmāda, Apasmāra, Vātavyādhis, Vātarakta, Ūrustambha, Āmavāta, Šūla, Paktija Sūla, Ānāha, Udāvarta, Gulmaroga, Hrdroga, Mūtrakrchra, Mītrāghāta, Ašmarī, Prameha, Madhumeha, Prameha Pidakās, Medo roga, Udara roga, Šotha, Vrddhi, Galaganda, Gandamālā, Apacī, Granthi, Arbuda, Šīlpada, Vidradhi, Vranasotha, Two kinds of Vranas, Bhagna, Naḍīvraņa, Bhagandara, Upadamša, Šūka Bossa, Twak rogas ( kuştha ), Šitapitta, Udarda, Sitagarada, Masūrikā, Romāntikā, Ksudra rogas, Āsyarogas, Karņa rogas, Nasā rogas, Aksi rogas, Siro rogas, Stri rogas, Bāla rogas and Viṣa rogas are the contents of this treatise-Rugviniscaya. ( 1-9 )

# सुभाषितं यत्र यदस्ति किंचित्तरसर्वेमेकोकृतमन्त्र यद्मात् । विनिश्चये सर्वर्ता नराणां भीमाधवेनेन्दुकरात्मजेद ॥ १० ॥

The best descriptions found in different places ( texts ) have been compiled here with great effort, by Sri Mādhava son of Indukara, for understanding of all diseases, man is heir to. ( 10 )

> यत्कृतं सुरूतं किंचित्कृत्वैवं द्वग्वनिश्चयम् । जन्तवस्तेन नित्यमात्रङ्कसन्ततिम् ॥ ११ ॥ मुश्चन्तु

> > इति श्रीमाधवकरविरचितं माधवनिदानं समाप्तम् ॥

With the hope that "Any work done with good intention is bound to be good" this Rugviniscaya has been written; by its help, let all beings get rid of the chain of diseases permanently.

Thus ends Roga Viniscaya of Mādhavakara.

244

#### APPENDIX 1

# SOME SALIENT POINTS FROM MADHUKOŚA VYAKHYA

#### Chapter 1-Pañca Nidana Laksanam

Sloka No. 4-Nidāna, Pūrvarūpa, Rūpa, Upasaya and Samprāpti-these five help in the diagnosis of diseases, each one separately and all of them collectively. Even though a disease can be understood by anyone, it is better to make use of the others also in view of many advantages :

If Nidana ( cause ) is not mentioned, how can any one be able to 'avoid the cause' ? 'Avoiding the cause' is a part of treatment itself. Hence its mention in diseases, for example, eating mud as a cause for Pandu roga ( anaemia ), swallowing a fly as a cause for Chardi ( vomitting ) etc. Hence knowledge of Nidana is essential.

It will not be possible to understand all diseases by Nidana ( cause ) only because it may be the same for many diseases; for example, jwara (fever) and gulma ( abdominal tumor ); sometimes the nearest cause might not produce the disease but a remote cause ( not mentioned in the text ) might have produeed it ... Hence Vapyacandra stresses the necessity of mentioning Purvarupa ( prodromal symptoms ) etc. If Purvarupa is not described; ... the difference between Raktapitta and Prameha cannot be known because in both Prameha and Raktapitta the yellow coloured urine is found; but in Raktapitta the purvarupa (charectoristic prodromata ) of Prameha are absent.

If Rupa is not described, then no knowledge of the disease, its curability or incuribility, etc., is possible. If Upasaya is not described it will be impossible to diagnose diseases which have too many symptoms either mixed up or concealed. If Samprapti is not described, it will not be possible to know the dosas and dhatus involved, the time of exacerbation or mitigation etc. and to plan proper treatment.

So all these five are to be described in the text for better understanding of diseases. Hence all the five are together known as 'Nidana', in the sense "पञ्चविध मण्येतत् व्याध्युत्पत्ति श्रसि हेतु भूतं निद्दान शब्देनोच्यते''. Susruta has said, "हेतु लक्षणनिर्देशात् निद्दानानि" and the definition of Nidāna will be "निर्दिंਝयते ब्याधिरनैनैति निदानम्". The term Nidana is applicable to the to mean both the cause and also to this group of five. It is also applicable to the text which describes diseases in these five aspects.

Sloka 5-Hetū ( cause ) are of different kinds as follows : I—According to Haricandra they are of four types, viz, Sannikrşṭa, Viprakṛṣṭa, Vyabhicāri and Prādhānika.

a ) Sannikrsta ( nearest, immediate )--the time of night and day and of food f etc, causing Prakopa (increase) of dosas quickly without the stage of Claya; likewise the use of Ruksa foods as the cause of Jwara ( fever )

b ) Viprakrṣṭa ( farthest, remote )——Kapha undergoing increase in Hemanta rtu causing a Kapharoga in Vasanta rtu (after a lapse of two months) or Rudrakopa as a cause of Jwara ( fever ).

c ) Vyabhicāri { mild, inefficient } incapable of producing the disease because of poor strength, and requiring help of other causes of

d ) Prādhānika ( strong, efficient, potent ) producing discase by itself quickly, as for example, Visa ( poison ).

II—Three-fold causes viz., Asatmyendi iyartha Samyoga, Ba Prajñäparādha and Pariņāma.

a ) Asätmyendriyarthasanyoga--Improper correlation ( hyper, hypo or perverse ) of the senses with their objects.

b ) Prajñāparādha—Violation of the usual physical, mental and vocal activities, because of improper understanding.

c ) Parināma-Hyper, hypo or perverse features of seasons.

III-Another three-fold cause, viz., Dosa hetu, Vyādhi hetu and Ubhaya hetu.

a ) Dosa hetu—Ingestion of Madhurarasa,etc., causing the Prakopa (increase) of doşas primarily,

b ) Vyādhi hetu-Eating mud as causing Panduroga, primarily.

c ) Ubhaya hetu---Riding on elephants, horses, camel etc. for long periods and also indulging in vidāhi foods together cause Vatarakta.

IV—Two-fold causes, viz , Utpadaka and Vyañjaka

a ) Utpadaka ( primary, potent )—Use of Madhura rasa in Hemanta rtu causing Prakopa of Kapha independently.

b ) Vyarijaka ( secondary, assisting )-The heat of the sun in Vasanta rtu causing Prakopa of Kapha, acting as exciting cause. V-Another two-fold cause, viz., Bahya and Abhyantara.

a ) Bahya ( external )-Foods, activities, seasons, etc.,

b ) Abhyantara ( internal )-The dosas and dusyas.

VI-Another two-fold cause for Dosa prakopa, (increase) viz., Prakrta and Vaikrta.

a ) Prākṛta ( normal, usual )-Prakopa of Kapha in Vasanta, Pitta in Sarat and Vata in Pravrt, rtus are normal.

Vaikṛta ( abnormal, unusual )—Prakopa of Pitta or Vātā in Vasanta, Kapha or pitta in Varsā; Kapha or Vāta in Šarat ruus are unusual to these seasons.

VII-Another two fold cause, viz., Anubandhya, Anubandha.

a } Anubandhya-Powerful. independent, efficient.

- b ) Anubandha-mild, follower, inefficient.
VIII-Yet another two fold cause viz.,

a ) Prakrti ( Constitutional )—Vātarogasin persons of Vātaprakrti are incurable.

b ) Vikrti ( non-constitūtional )-- the same Vātarogas in persons of Kapha or Pitta Prakrti are easily curable.

IX-Asayāpakarṣa ( dislodgement ) is also a cause for disease-the doşa though in its normal quantity, if driven away from its normal site to another site by vata, tends to produce diseases.

X-Gati ( state ) of dosas-is also a cause of disease, the state of dosas are three.

- a ) Sthāna—normal
- b ) Vrddhi-increase
- c ) Kṣaya-decrease,

All the above causes are summarised in the following verses-

```
चत्वारो व्यभिचारि दूर्रनिकट प्राधानिकत्वात् पुनः
           तेऽसात्म्येन्द्रियकार्थयुक्तपरिणति प्रक्षापराधान्धित्रधा ।
  म्दोषोभयकारणाद्रपि तथा,
                                  द्धौव्य अकोत्पाद कौ
           बाह्यान्यन्तर मेदतोऽपि कथिता हेतोः प्रभेद्दा अमी ।।
दोषस्य च प्राकृतवैकृताध्यां मेदोऽनुबन्ध्याद्वपि चानुबन्धात् ।
          प्रकृत्यप्रकृतित्वयोगात्तथाऽऽशयाक्कर्षवशाद्भवश्
तथा
                                                           料 ||
```
b ) Vyarijaka ( secondary, assisting, exciting )-The heat of the sun in Vasanta rtu causing Prakopa of Kapha, acting as exciting cause. V-Another two-fold cause, viz., Bahya and Abhyantara.

a ) Bahya ( external )-Foods, activities, seasons, etc.,

b } Abhyantara { internal }-The dosas and dusyas.

VI-Another two-fold cause for Dosa prakopa, (increase) viz., Prakrta and Vaikrta.

a ) Prākṛta ( normal, usual )-Prakopa of Kapha in Vasanta, Pitta in Sarat and Vata in Pravrt, rtus are normal.

Vaikṛta ( abnormal, unusual )—Prakopa of Pitta or Vāṭa in Vasanta, Kapha or pitta in Varsā; Kapha or Vāta in Šarat rius are unusual to these seasons,

VII-Another two fold cause, viz., Anubandhya, Anubandha.

a } Anubandhya-Powerful. independent, efficient.

- b ) Anubandha-mild, follower, inefficient.
VIII-Yet another two fold cause viz.,

a ) Prakrti ( Constitutional )—Vātarogasin persons of Vātaprakrti are incurable.

b ) Vikrti ( non-constitūtional )-- the same Vātarogas in persons of Kapha or Pitta Prakrti are easily curable.

IX-Asayāpakarṣa ( dislodgement ) is also a cause for disease-the dosa though in its normal quantity, if driven away from its normal site to another site by vata, tends to produce diseases.

X-Gati ( state ) of dosas-is also a cause of disease, the state of dosas are three.

- a ) Sthāna—normal
- b ) Vrddhi-increase
- c ) Kṣaya-decrease,

All the above causes are summarised in the following verses-

चत्वारो व्यभिचारि दूर्रनिकट प्राधानिकत्वात् पुनः तेऽसात्म्येन्द्रियकार्थयुक्तपरिणति प्रक्षापराधान्धित्रधा । म्दोषोभयकारणाद्रपि तथा, द्वौव्य अकोत्पाद कौ बाह्याभ्यन्तर मेदतोऽपि कथिता हेतोः प्रभेद्दा अमी ।। दोषस्य च प्राकृतवैकृताभ्यां मेदोऽनुबन्ध्याद्यपि चानुबन्धात् । प्रकृत्यप्रकृतित्वयोगात्तथाऽऽशयाकर्षवशाद्भवश्राद्भतेः तथा 科 ||

Sloka 6-Pūrvarūpa is of two kinds; Samānya ( general ) and Višista ( special, peculiar ).

Sāmanya Pūrvarūpa is defined as—

# "येन दोषदूध्य संम्मूर्छनावस्था जनितेन भावि ज्वरादि मान्नं प्रतीयते न तु वातादि जनितत्वादि विशोषः ॥'''

The symptoms produced by the interaction of abnormal dosas and dusyas, which indicate the future disease but not its dosic variety.

Višišţa Pūrvarūpa is defined as-

# अव्यक्तं लक्षणं तस्य पूर्वरूपमिति स्मृतम् ।

The very same symptoms of the future disease manifesting early in a mild form is called as Visista Purvarüpa; as in the case of Uraksata, the symptoms of increased Vāta alone manifest. The symptoms of Sāmānya Pūrvarūpa need not necessarily be present in the Rupa stage whereas the symptoms of Visista Purvarupa, themselves become powerful and manifest as symptoms in the Rupa stage.

Some others define Parvarüpa as follows-

# स्थानसंश्रयिणः क्रुद्धा भावि व्याधि प्रबोधकम् । दोषाः कुर्वन्ति यूर्यन्त्रिङ्गं पूर्वरूपं तदुन्द्रयते ।।

"The symptoms produced by the dosas which have undergone increase and attained the stage of sthanasamsraya and heralding the future disease, are known as Pūrvarūpa."

"This definition is not appropriate; because the secing of grass, hairs, worms, etc., in the food by a patient of Rajayaksma, even though they are actually not present there, cannot be said as due to the dosas."

Sloka 7-A disease is understood by its symptoms, if no symptoms are found there is no disease at all; Aruci ( anorexia ) and other symptoms together is Jwara (fever ); Kāsa ( cough ) and ten other symptoms together is Rājayaksma ( pulmonary tuberculosis )-is the argument of some. But, it is not correct; Jwara and other diseases, are the products of interaction of dosas and dusyas; while Aruchi ( anorexia ) and other symptoms are the effects of that disease; Mere groups of symptoms are not diseases, abnormal changes in the dhatus causing duhkha ( discomfort ) is only to be taken as the disease. Aruci ( anorexia ) etc. are also diseases but when they indicate other diseases, they are known as Laksana (symnt oms ) only and not as Amayas ( diseases ),

Sloka 8-9-According to Vapyacandra, the two terms Dosapratyanika and Vyadhipratyanika are to be distinguished as follows :

#### यहो घटनावनीकं स्थान तन्नावर्त्यं व्याधिद्वरम् ।

-that which mitigates the dosas, need not mitigate the diseases. For example, Vamana ( emesis ) and Langhana ( fasting ) though Kaphahara do not cure Kapha Gulma.

#### व्याधिहरू तदवर्यं दोषहरम् । यत्त

-that which cures the disease does certainly mitigate the dosas, for, when the disease is cured the dosas which started it should also have been mitigated.

According to others this proposition is not correct. Because it is not definite whether the dosas are Samavayi ( inherent cause ) or Nimitta ( exciting, secondary, cause ). Union of many pieces of potsherds even though Asamavāyi ( non-inherent cause ), when destroyed, causes the absence of the pot; Similarly the destruction of the union of Laksanas ( symptoms ) and Samprapti ( pathogenesis ) causes the absence of the disease. The dosas get mitigated either by themselves or by other treatments. If we accept that Vyadhipratyanika drugs will also mitigate the dosas necessarily, then the usage of the term, Ubhayapratyanika becomes redundent. So the dosas are not Samavayi. If dosas are considered as Nimitta Kāraņa, how can we accept that dosas are removed out of the body by Vamana ( emesis ) etc. Absence of the stick or the potter does not cause the absence or destruction of the pot. So it can be said that so far as an effect is solely dependent on the Nimitta Karanas, destruction or absence of such Kāraņas makes for loss or absence of the effect; for example, the loss or absence of the wick and oil causes absence or loss of the flame; likewise also is the role of the dosas in relation to disease.

The following are the different kinds of Upaśayas along with examples : I. Viparita-( opposite ).

A ) Hetuviparīta ( opposite of the cause )

- ) use of Sunti which is ushna virya ( hot in potency ) in a ) Ousadha ( drug ) | Jwara ( fever ) caused by shita ( cold )
- use of Mamsarasa and rice in fever caused by Vāta due b ) Anna ( food ) to over-exertion.
- c ) Vihāra Avoiding sleep at night to reduce the over accumulated kapha due to sleeping during day. (activity)
- B ) Vyadhi Vipartta ( opposite of the disease )
	- a ) Ousadha-Use of Pāṭhā which is sthambhana in the disease Atisāra; so also Sirisa in Visa, Khadira in Kustha. etc.
	- b ) Anna --- Use of soup of Masūra dhal in the disease Atisāra;
	- c ) Vihāra Pravāhaņa ( straining ) in the disease Udāvarta; use of mantra, mani ousadha, homa, bali, yajña, prāyascitta, etc., in other diseases.
- C ) Ubhaya Viparita ( opposite of both cause and disease )
	- a ) Ouṣadha—Use of Daṣamឋla in Vātajaśotha which is both Vātahara and Sothahara.
	- -Use of Takra in Vātakapha Grahaņī. b ) Anna
	- c } Vihara -- Avoiding sleep at night to mitigate the drowsiness caused by day-sleep and fatty foods.
- II. Viparita Arthakāri (producing the opposite effect though not actually opposite ).
	- A ) Hetuviparītārthakāri
		- a ) Oușadha—Application of hot poultice in a case of Vrațasotha caused by pitta.
		- b } Anna Use of Vidāhi foods in case of Pacyamana Vraņašotha.
		- c ) Vihāra Threatening the patient suffering from Vātaja Unmāda.
	- B \ Vyādhi Viparītārthakāri
		- a } Ousadha-Use of Madanaphala, an emetic drug in case of Chardi ( vomitting )
		- -Use of milk which is a purgative in case of Atisara b ) Anna ( diarrhoea )
		- c } Vihara -- Pravahana ( straining ) in case of Chardi { vomitting } to induce more vomittings.
	- C ) Ubhaya Viparītārthakāri
		- a ) Ousadha—Application of a paste of Aguru which is of ushaguna on burns caused by fire.
		- -- Use of intoxicating drinks in cases of Madātvaya b ) Anna ( alcoholism ).

- c ) Vihāra -Mild exercises like swimming, in cases of stiffness of the legs caused by over-exertion.
Sloka 12-Amsāmša Kalpanā is explained as follows :

rūkṣa, śīta, laghu, vaiśadya, etc., are the properties of Vātā. Vāta might get increased by one or more of such properties. For example, the use of kasaya rasa and kalāya ( peas ) which possess all the properties of vāta might cause Vātavrddhi, then it is Vātavrddhi by all its properties. Taņḍulīyaka causes Vātavrddhi by three gunas, viz, rūkṣa, sīta and laghu; Kāndekṣu by two gunas viz., rūkṣa and śita; Sidhu by only rūkṣa guna; similarly kaṭu rasa and madya possessing all the properties of pitta, make for its increase in all its qualities. Hingu by katu, uṣṇa and tīkṣṇa gunas; Dīpyːka by tīkṣṇa and usna gunas and Tila by only usnaguna. Likewise madhura rasa and buffalo's milk cause increase of Kapha by all its properties, fruit of Rājādana by its sneha, gourava and mādhurya; Kaśeru by its saitya and gourava, fruits of Kṣīra Vrksas by śaitya only.

Well; while enumerating the varieties of Samprapti, Caraka has mentioned Vidhi Samprapti. How is it that Vidhi Samprapti is not mentioned here ? The answer for this is, Vidhi is included in Sankhya Samprāpti itself; Vidhi indicates varieties within anyone thing; for eg. Urdhwaga and Adhoga kinds in the disease Raktapitta; whercas Saikhya indicates number of different kinds, eg : Jwara is of eight kinds, etc.

Sloka 13-14-The causes of any disease are two :

a } Viprakrsta = farthest, remote, distant } which are the unsuitable foods and activities, b ) Sannikrsta ( nearest, immediate ) that is the dosas-vatia, roitta and kapha. These are essential causes ( Avyabhicarita kāraņa ) in all disease prilu case of Aganturogas, though dosaprakopa is not present at the commencement it is sure to develop soon.

Well : what is this 'दोषत्व' of Vata and other dosas ? To this question, some say ''स्वातन्त्र्येण दूषकरचं दोषत्वम्'—it is their ability to vitiate others independrently, The term स्वातन्त्र्यण ( independently ) is to exclude Rasa and other dhatus they vitiate each other only after getting vitiated by the dosas. To this term, some may question, is it independence devoid of any relation between one another on is it independence devoid of relationship with other causes ? If it is the first meaning, then Vata only will have to be called dosa because of its ability to vitiate independently but not the other two doşas because they act as directed by Vata. If it is the second meaning even Vata can not be a dosa because, it also requires other causes to become vitiated, Hence 'दोष्टच' is to be defined as "प्रकृत्यारम्भकर्त्वे सति दुष्टि कर्तूलं दोषत्वम"-The tendency to cause vitiation even though they are the progenitors of the constitution ( of the human being ) ...

The differance between, Prakrti { normal constitution, the dosa responsible to produce it } and Roga ( disease, the dosa which produces it ) is; ( the dosa of the ) Prakrti does not give troubles to the body inspite of the use of unwholesome food, etc. just as a worm born in a poisonous material does not die of that poison, so also the body does not suffer or perish.

Well; if the dosas are the causes of diseases, then man should have been always a sick person because the dosas are always present in the body. It is not so, because it is specified that "only when they get aggravated ( increased ) they cause diseases''.

Well; is their ( dosas ) becoming aggravated natural ( of their own accord ) or due to other causes ? ( The answer to this is ) it is not the former but only the latter i. e. only due to other causes. What are these other causes ? They are the various unhealthy foods and activities under the three headings, vig. Asatmyendriyārtha samyoga, Prajñāparādha and Pariņāma.

#### Chapter 2-Jwara Nidana

Sloka 1-Rudrakopa ( anger of god Rudra ) is mentioned here as the cause of [wara ( fever ), Since it is produced by anger it is Agneya ( hot ) and involvement of Pitta dosa is to be implied thereby. Secondly, because it is of divine origin it deserves worship also in the form of an image ( of fever god ), as a part of its treatment.

Sloka 2 : 'Mithyahara' means foods which are not in accordance with the eight kinds of qualities of foods mentioned by Caraka; such as 'प्रकृतिकरण संयोगराशि-देश काल्कोपयोगसंस्थोपयोकव्रण्रमानि' ( च. वि. ? ).

The use of the term 'आमाश्रयाश्रया:' is to indicate that Dosas will not produce Jwara unless they come to Amasaya ( recepticle of undigested food, stomach ). For the increase of temperature of the body, the cause is kosthagni ( digestive fire ) itself now displaced.

Sloka 14-When two or all the three dosas combine together to produce a disease, the symptoms manifesting thereon will be of two types viz.,

- a ) Prakṛti Sama Samavāya Lakṣaṇas--the effect being similar in nature to the cause. eg. white colour of a cloth weaved with white coloured yarn. The charectorestic features of the combining dosas being seen as symptoms of the disease.
- b ) Vikiti Viṣama Samavāya Lakṣaṇas-effect being different in nature to the cause, eg; red colour produced by the combination of yellow coloured turmeric powder and white coloured limewater. The symptoms of the disease not assignable to the combining dosas.

( In the texts, Dwandwa and Sannipata varieties are described only when Vikrtivisama Samavāya Laksaņas are seen to develop in the course of the disease. }

Slokas 18-23-1'he three dosas are having properties opposite of one another. So each one should destroy the other as seen in the case of water and fire. How do they produce Jwara and other diseases ? The answer to the question is furnished by Dhrdabala who says-

## विरुद्धरणि न रवेते ग्रुणैध्ननित परस्परम् । दोषाः सहजसात्म्यत्वाद् विषं घोर महोनिव ॥ ( च. चि. २६ )

Dosas, even though they are opposite of one another, do not undergo mutual destruction because they have a natural immunity; similar to the presence of poison in the snake does not kill the snake.

Cakrapāņi also states Sahajasātmya ( natural immunity ) as the lone cause but Gayadasa adds Daiva ( providence ) also and states as follows-

## देवाद्योषस्वभावाद्धा दोषाणां सान्निपातिके। विरुद्धः स्वगुणैः कश्चिन्नोपधातः परस्परम् ॥

Sloka 31-The word Alpa' here denotes 'poor strength' of the dosas, and so waits for time, etc., to produce the disease Twara { fever },

Sloka 34-35-Viṣama Jwarās are so called because they have both Mukti ( relief ) and Anubandha ( recurrence ) during their course.

Slokas 61-62-Samata ( presence of Tma ) is of two kinds, viz. ( a ) that in the Rasadhātu indicated by symptoms like bad taste in the mouth, etc., and ( b ) that in the Dosas indicated by the early stages of disease. The purpose of knowing them distinctly is to plan out treatment, if Samata is present in the dogas ( as in early stages of discase ) no mcdicine need be administered but if it is found in Rasadhatu, then pacana drugs have to be given.

Slokas 66-73 - Jwara and other diseases either cause death of the patient or get cured by two processes, viz Dhātupāka and Doṣapāka ( malapāka ); the former causes death and the latter produces cure of the disease; occurrence of either Dhātupāka or Malapāka is dependent upon Daiva ( providence ). A steady increase of severity of the disease and decrease of strength and quantity of the dhatus ( tissues ), urine, facces, etc., are the symptoms of Dhātupāka, Further, also

## निद्रानाशो हदिस्तम्भो विष्टम्भो गौरवाऽद्वी । अरतिबंलहानिश्च धातूनां पाक लक्षणम् ।।

Well; Diseases will not disappear without the decrease of the dosas. Then, how does a dosa which is very weak by decrease, produce powerful symptoms at the terminal stages of the disease ? It is possible; sometimes a very weak object exhibits its full strength at its end; as seen in the flame burning brightest at its end. It may occur in another way, the symptoms like daha, ksobha, etc., might occur when the dosas suddenly get out of the dhatus, as seen in the example, the top portion of a slender tree swings very much even if a little monkey jumps from it to another nearby tree.

#### Chapter 3-Atīsāra Nidānam

Sloka 4-Ap dhātu here means Rasa, Jala, Mūtra, Sweda, Medas, Kapha, Pitta, Rakta and all others which are fluid in nature,

Well, the sixth kind of Atisara is named here as Amatisara separately. It is said that the carly stages of all varieties of Atisara is to be considered as Amatisara and the later stages as Pakwatisara. Then why a separate classification as Amatisara ? The answer to this will be that Ama here is indicative of improperly digested food associated with morbid dosas and dhatus. This Ama by itself is capable of causing Atisara independent of dosas. Hence a separate classification.

Sloka 21-The difference between Atisara and Pravāhikā is that in Atīsāra, different kinds of liquids ( tissues, wastes, etc., ) are eliminated whereas in Pravahika only kapha is expelled out. अतीसारे नानाविध द्रव धातु सरणम् प्रवाद्विकायां तु कफमात्रसरणमिति भेदः ).

#### Chapter 4-Grahani Roga Nidanam

Sloka 11-12-Pitta is Agneya or in other words Agni itself. Then how is it said here that Agni is diminished in case of Pittaja Grahani, in which pitta should have necessarily undergone vrddhi to produce that disease ? The answer is clear from the example of hot water which can extinguish fire. Here pitta undergoes vrddhi only in its dravāmsa ( fluidity ) which in · turn diminishes the usma ( uṣṇa and tiksna ) guna of Agni which is necessary for digestion. Hence it is described as diminishing the Agni.

Sloka 17-Bv Grahani dusti ( abnormality of grahani ) is implied the dusti of Agni ( abnormality of digestive capacity ) also since Agni is located in grahani.

Chapter 5- Agnimāndya Ajīrna Nidānam

Slokas 5-6-Gadadhara says of Rasasesa Ajirna as,

रसे शेषो रसशेष:, आद्वार जनिते रखे शेष आहारावयवो अनुप्रविष्टोऽल्क्ष्यमाण: क्षीरनीरमिव रस शेषः --

Well; What is the difference between other Ajirnas and this ? The other two are produced by food, whereas Rasasesa Ajirna is from the end product of digestion.

Sloka 21-In both, Alasaka and Vilambika the symptoms such as nonmovement of food either upwards or downwards is common. Then, what is the difference between the two ? In Alasaka, there will be severe pain in the abdomen, etc., while in Vilambika it is not so.

#### Chapter 8-Panduroga Nidānam

Sloka 1 -Paṇḍuroga is the disease charectorised by pāṇḍutwa पाण्ड्रत्वे नोपलक्षितो रोग: वाण्डरोग: - ( Pandutwa is morbid pallor, yellowish-white discolouration ).

Sloka 18-Kamala is of two kinds viz. a }, kosthasraya-localised in the kostha ( abdomen ) and b ) sakhāsraya-residing in the sakhas ( tissues ).

Kamala can also occur independently.

#### Chapter 9-Raktapitta Nidānam

Sloka 1-2-Is Rakta vitiated by Pitta-the abnormality in Raktapitta. If that is the case then, the name should have been Pitta rakta. Or is it that the vitiated Rakta and vitiated Pitta together cause Raktapitta. It is the latter that should be accepted.

#### Chapter 10-Rājayaksmā Nidānam

Sloka 1-The four causitive factors like Vegarodha, etc., are going to increase Vāta dosa only. Then how Rājayakşmā is described as Tridoṣaja ? It can be stated, in answer to the question, that Agni undergoes abnormality because of Vitaprakopa and next, the other two dosas also undergo Prakopa due to Agnidusți. Hence Rājayakşma becomes Tridoşaja.

Sloka 2 - Dhātukṣaya can take place in two ways viz., a ) Anuloma ( downward )--- starting from loss of Rasa all the succeeding dhātus undergo wasting, b ) Pratiloma ( upward )-starting from sukra all the preceding dhātus undergo wasting. Can Pratiloma be accepted ? How can we say that destruction of the effect will destroy the cause ? The answer to the objection will be that Sukra kṣaya causes Vātavṛddhi and that in turn, causes wasting of Majjā and so on. An example can be given of "a heated iron ball which dries up the moist mud surrounding it."

#### Chapter 11-Kāsa Nidānam

Sloka 1-The term 'Kasa' is derived from the verb 'kas' meaning 'gati ( movement ), कस्म गतौ इत्यस्मात् कसति शिरः कण्ठादूध्वे भव्छति वायुरिति कासः ( Vata moves upwards to the throat and head ) will be the definition of Kasa.

#### Chapter 14-Arocaka Nidanam

Sloka 4-Both Caraka and Susruta have grouped three conditions under the term Arocaka, viz.,

- a ) Aruci-inability to eat even though a person has got hunger, ( loss of taste ).
- b ) Anannābhinandana—inability to eat with relish even though supplied with food that is liked ( loss of appetite ).
- c ) Bhaktadwesa-hatredness towards food by even the sound, sight, smell and touch of food ( aversion to food ).

# Chapter 17-Mūrchādi Nidānam

 Sloka 1–4–Samjñavaha Nāḍīs here refers to the sira, dhamani and srotas
gh which the mind trevel stati through which the mind travels to the sense organs.

Sloka 13-Bhibhatsaceşta refers to appearance of froth in the mouth, grinding of teeth, abnormal gaze which precede unconsciousness and falling on the ground.

#### Chapter 20-Unmada Nidanam

Sicha 5-Manovaha Srotas are the ten dhamanis connected to the heart. Even though manas ( mind ) travels in all the srotas of the body, in the present context, it is the ten dhamanis.

Sloka 29 -The term 'Deva' here refers to the attendants of gods who have some qualities of gods, but not actually to the gods because they do not touch the unsacred human body. This is specifically stated by Susruta ( Su. Utt. 60 ) :

#### देव शब्देन चात्र देवस्यानुबरो देव सधर्माणो ग्रह्यन्ते. मनुष्य शरीरेणाशुचिना संबन्धाभावात 11 दवानां

#### Chapter 22-V atavyādhi Nidānam

Slokas 1-4-Why is this disease called as Vatavyādhi ? Is it that Vāta is the disease ? or is it the disease caused by . Vata ? If the first proposition is accepted then it becomes valid even for healthy state; if the second proposition is accepted, then Jwara and all other diseases are also caused by Vata. This anomoly can by solved by interpreting as "that disease which is caused by the abnormal ( increased ) Vata which produces severe discomforts "व्यधिपद समानाधिकरण्यात् विकृतो दुःखकारी वातो वातव्याधिः" इति, that peculiar disease caused by Vata is to be called as Vatavyādhi. "वातजनितोऽसाधारण व्याधिः वातव्याधिरिति विशेषणीयम्" ।

Caraka has classified diseases into two kinds : Samanyaja and Nanatmaja; Samanyaja are those diseases which are produced by one or more of the dosas like Jwara, etc, Nanatmaja are those which are produced specifically by any one dosa itself without the help of other dosas: For example Aksepaka, etc., are produced by Vata only, and not either by pitta or kapha; Osa and Cosa are produced only by pitta and not by vata or kapha; Tripti, etc., are produced by kapha only and not either by Vata or Pitta.

In the light of the above proposition, how is it that there is mention of Vatavyādhi but not Pittavyādhi or Kaphavyādhi ? The reason for this is, vāta being very powerful and quick acting, produces major diseases which are very troublesome, incurable, causing emergency danger to life, and requiring special kinds of treat-

ment, but the other two dosas do not have the above qualities; hence they are not described separately by both Caraka and Suśruta. Candrikakāra states thus -Pitta and Kapha possessing rūpa, rasa, etc., and combining with the dhātus produce symptoms which are different from their own characteristics. Just like combination of turmeric and lime water produces red colour. Vata on the other hand not possessing rūpa, rasa, etc., and not combining with the dhātus produces Āksepaka and such other symptoms which are not very different from its own properties and actions; hence a separate description for disorders of vāta but not for those of kapha or pitta separately.

## Chapter 23-Vātarakta Nidānam

Slokas 1-3-Severe pain, increase of heat, etc., being the symptoms of Rakta-gata vāta was described in the chapter of Vātavyādhi, why then a separate chapter as Vātarakta? It is not like that; Vātarakta is a distinct disease produced by increased vāta and vitiated rakta having a specific samprāpti. In Raktagatavāta, the change is only increase of vāta but rakta will be nomral. This is the difference between Vātarakta and Raktagatavāta.

## Chapter 25-Amavāta Nidānam

Ślokas 1-5—Ama is improperly digested food materials remaining over, in the end product of digestion. It is described by different authorities as follow.

| ऊष्मणोऽ ल्यबलत्वेन |    | धातुमाद्यमपाचितम् । |                              |
|--------------------|----|---------------------|------------------------------|
| दुष्टमामांशय       | म् | रसमाम               | प्रचक्षते ।। (अ. हृ. सू. १३) |

The first dhātu (Rasa) not properly digested (processed) because of the weakness of the Usma (Jatharagni-the digestive activity in the alimentary tract) accumulating in the Āmāšaya (stomach and intestines) in known as Āma.

```
आमाशयस्यो कायाग्रे दौर्बलयादविपाचितः ।
आद्याऽहारधातुर्यः स आम इति कीर्तितः ।।
```
The meaning of this verse is same as above.

```
अविपक्कमसंयुक्तं दुर्गन्थं बहुपिच्छिलम् ।
सादनं सर्वगात्राणां आम इत्याभिधीयते ।।
```
Undigested, improperly processed, foul smelling, very sticky material causing weakness (or other disorders) of all the parts of the body is spoken of as Ama.

#### आहारस्य रस: श्रोषो यो न पक्कोऽप्रिलाधवात् । सर्वरोगाणां आम इत्यभिधीयते ।। मल स्

The remnant of the food, not properly digested because of the poor Agni, which becomes the cause of all diseases is known as Ama.

# आममन्नरसं केचित् केचित् मल सञ्चयम् । प्रथमां दोष दुष्टिं च केचिदामं प्रचक्षते ।।

Some say that Ama is the end product of food (improperly digested), others say it is accumulation of waste products, yet others, that it is the primary increase of Dosas.

#### Chapter 28-Gulma Nidānam

Slokas 17-20-Both Antar-Vidradhi and Gulma develop in the same place; then what is the difference between them ? The difference is, Vidradhi undergoes pāka (pus formation) while Gulma does not, because it is not having any recepticle (tissue to support) : Again, another piont of order; Caraka has said (Ca. Ci. 5) that Gulma also undergoes pus formation. Then the answer will be; sometime due to the effect of the exciting cause it finds a dwelling in the tissues like Mamsa, etc., Then treatments such as swedana (sudation), etc., done to mitigate vāta, produce Raktadusți (vitiation of rakta) and causes an abcess in which pus formation might happen. Hence the proposition that Vidradhi undergoes paka (pus formation) but not Gulma.

#### Chapter 31-Mūtrāghāta Nidānam

Sloka 1-The difference between mutrakrchra and Mutraghata is, in Mutrakrchra, difficulty to eliminate urine is great and obstruction is less whereas in mūtrāghāta obstruction is more and difficulty of elimination is very less.

#### Chapter 33-Prameha Nidānam

Sloka 2-3-The term क्षीणदोष in this verse should not be taken to mean decrease of dosas less than normal but less in quantity as compared to the increased ones (dosas), such as kapha, pitta and even other dhatus involved.

Samakriyatwa means similarity between kapha the dosa ( the vitiator ) and medas, etc., the dusyas (the vitiated) in respect of their qualities, treatment and the use of Kaju, Tikta and Kasaya drugs acting on both the dosas and dusyas effectively.

Viṣamakriyatwa means dissimilarity between pitta the doṣa ( vitiator ) and medas etc., the dusya ( the vitiated ) in respect of their qualities and treatment, Madhura rasa drugs administered to mitigate pitta cause the increase of the dusya like medas; drugs containing katu rasa, etc., administered to decrease medas, etc., cause the increase of pitta the dosa, so the difficulty in treatment.

Sloka 22-The term 'Kulaja' means hereditary or inherited from father or grand-father ( paternal ) or maternal. If the grandfather is a pramehi ( a person suffering from polyuria or diabetes ) will his son also be born as a pramehi ? No. The son of such a person need not be a diabetic by birth itself. He may aquire prameha ( diabetes ) anytime in his life by indulgence in the causes of diabetes.

Sloka 36-Some opine that women do not become victims of prameha ( diabetes ) because they get rid of dosas every month by the menstrual flow. This view is incorrect as it is contrary to reality. Women also suffer from prameha ( diabetes ).

## Chapter 34-Medoroga Nidānam

Sloka 1-9-Persons suffering from Medoroga are described as having TIksnagni ( hyperactive digestion ), then how it is said that in those persons the food will all be turned into madhura ( sweet ) which in other words means Ama ? The answer to this doubt is that Ama is brought about by ingestion of foods causing Kapha vrddhi ( increase of kapha ) in large quantities or irregularity in respect of time of food, etc., will cause for production of Ama Or all the cham els ( or pores ) of the alimentary tract become clogged with juice of sweet foods, so much, that all the other foods containing other rasas ( tastes ) will also get converted to sweetness only which may lead to Ama accumulation.

#### Chapter 44-Bhagna Nidanam

Sloka 1 -- Bhagnas ( fractures ) are of two kinds viz. a ) Savraņa ( with a wound and b ) Avrana ( without a wound ).

Kanda bhagna might happen in all the five kinds of bones.

#### Chapter 49-Kustha Nidanam

Sloka 1-6 -It is seen that the dosas and dusyas in Visarpa and Kustha are the same. Then what is the difference between them ? The answer is "Kuştha is a disease of long duration caused by hard and less powerful vitiation of rakta and pitta. Whereas as Visarpa is quick manifesting ( spreading ) and caused by very profound vitiation of rakta and pitta; insulting elders and stealing the wealth of others are mentioned as the causes of Kustha but these are not mentioned as the cause of Visarpa.

Slokas 17-22 -- It is seen that Caraka and Susruta have no uniformity in classifying the kinds of Kustha; for example, Caraka includes Sidhma in Mahakusthas while Susruta puts it in Ksudrakusthas. Why this differance ? Gadadhara states that a deep rooted Sidhma is included in Mahakustha by Caraka whereas Susruta includes Sidhmapuspika which is not deep-rooted, under Ksudrakustha (the criteria of classification is the nature of symptoms ), Jejjata explains that the same disease is given different names by different scholars, eg : the very same Sidhma of Caraka is called as Dadru by Susruta. Naming differently is the cause for difference of opinion. Both the above views are not acceptable because there are many other differences also with regard to Kuştha. For example Arunakuştha mentioned by Susruta is not in Caraka, Mandalakustha of Caraka is not found in Sustuta; Ksudrakusthas like Carmākhya, Alasaka, Satāru, etc., enumerated by Caraka are not mentioned by Susruta, while Rakasa, etc., of Susruta are not found in Caraka. Hence it is wise to say that Kuştha being a disease of a large number of varieties some are mentioned by Susruta and some others by Caraka.

Stokas 37-39 The disease Kilasa is included in this chapter itself because of similarity in their causes and methods of treatment.

Kilasa is called Aruna and Switea Aruna and Switra when residing in mamsa and medas dhātus respectively. Kilāsa is mainly located in the twak (skin), hence it is described as non-exudative. Exudation occurs only when the deeper tissues like rakta, māmsa and medas are affected.

#### Chapter 55-Ksudraroga Nidānam

On what basis are the diseases in this chapter termed as 'Ksudra' ? Sloka 1 if it is on their few number of causes, mild and few symptoms and simple treatments, diseases like Agnirohini and Valmika, etc., cannot be considered under

this group; some say, because these diseases are not very common, they are called k sudra; some others opine, because there are not many varieties in these diseases as found in Vrana, Jwara, etc.; even if there are varieties their number being few, these diseases are called as Ksudra; some other say; that the term 'K sudra' means both alpa (few) and roudra (dreadful); yet others opine, K sudra is to be understood as applicable to children and all these are diseases of children; if this meaning is accepted diseases other than Ajagallikā, Ahipūtana, etc., have to be eliminated from this group.

Ślokas 32-In this verse, it is said, that both śarīrosmā and Pitta together will make the hairs grey; why this anomaly when it has been repeatedly declared by all the authorities, that pitta itself is Agni and apart from pitta there is no other agni in the body; in conditions of weakness of Agni, drugs like marica which are pittakara help to increase the functions of agni and drugs possessing sitaguna and madhurarasa which mitigate pitta make for decrease of the functions of agni; if we accept pitta and agni are different, then, the above activities of drugs cannot be explained; existence of hot things in the moon nor cold things in the sun can never be imagined. So it is a mistake here to have mentioned both agni and pitta separately.

As a counter to the above argument it can be explained as follows - even though there are many evidences to say that pitta and agni are one and the same, a subtle difference between the two has also been indicated by authorities in some places; for example in the statement समदोष: समाग्रि श्च------Pitta is included in the term dosa but still agni is mentioned separately; secondly, ghrita is going to lessen pitta but produces dipana (increases the activity of agni); peya and other foods increase agni but do not mitigate pitta; during the stage of pitta sañcaya (mild increase of pitta) there is no increase of agni -by all these, it can be assumed that Antaragni (internal fire, digestive fire) is different from pitta. Pitta is a product of combination of drava (fluid) and tejas (heat) and it is the tejas (heat) portion that should be understood as agni; it is not in the form of burning coal and ash as we find in the external fire. Because this tejas portion is present in pitta, categorically agni is also called as pitta; hence the statement that there is no agni other than pitta.

262

# Chapter 58—Nāsāroga Nidānam

Sloka 7-In this verse, prakopa of Kapha preceeded by sañcaya is mentioned, to differentiate it from the other kind i.e., prakopa without sañcaya; for it is said :

# न केवलं चयं प्राप्य दोषाः कुप्यन्ति देहिनाम् । अन्यतोऽहि कुर्प्यन्ति हेतु बाहुल्यतो बलात् ।।

It is not that the dosas undergo prakopa (profound increase) always preceded by sañcaya (mild increase); sometimes by the effect of more number or effective causes they undergo prakopa immediatly.

263

#### APPEŃDIX 11

#### MITHYA-AHARA-VIHARA

Ayurveda, emphatically states that, diseases are the outcome of indulgence in Mithya-Ahara-vihara or Ahita Aharavihara ( unwholesome or unhealthy foods and activities ). 'Ahara' means foods and drinks, and Vihara refers to all other human activities. They are mainly three viz ,

( a ) Kayaka/sariraka ( activities of the body including the five sense organs )

( b ) Manasika -activities of the mind.

( c ) Vacika-activities of speech.

Vihara also includes other factors such as effects of Kāla ( time ) the changes in the atmosphere during day, night and seasons. Thus, the scope of the term Mithyaharavihara is very wide and all its components have been visualised and explained with examples in detail in the Caraka Samhita, as the three chief causative factors of diseases, as follows --

- 1. Asatmyendriyartha Samyoga improper correlation of the five sense organs with their respective objects. It is also called 'Artha' in short.
- 2. Prajñaparadha …transgression or violation of usual or normal procedures of all activities, either by misunderstanding or ignorance. This is called as 'Karma' in short.
- 3. Parinama -is the effect of changes in the environment such as sunlight, breeze, cold etc., occuring during day, night and seasons. This is, in short called as 'Kāla'.

Each one of the above is having three Vikalpas ( subdivisions ) viz., ( a ) Atiyoga-excess ( indulgence ) { b ) Hīnayoga or Ayoga-deficient or absence ( of indulgence ) and ( c ) Mithyayoga-improper, unusual or irregular (indulgence). All these three are Visama or abnormal and tend to produce Roga ( disease. ) : There is another subdivision, the Samyagyoga -- the normal one-which is conducive to health or spoken of as Arogya ( health ) itself.

1. Ca. Su. 11 ; Ca. Sa. 1

All the three subdivisions of each of the three major heads, are eloborated in further pages. The explanation and examples found in the original texts and the commentary are given on the left hand side of the page and their modern parlance and few present-day examples are furnished on the right hand side, to fecilitate the present, day readers.
